component-export="Post" renderer-url="/_astro/client.Ck_OXNAA.js" props="{&quot;post&quot;:[0,{&quot;id&quot;:[0,&quot;xrcYtr7kU54LNDB8MEmQY&quot;],&quot;title&quot;:[0,&quot;Project Glasswing: what Mythos showed us&quot;],&quot;slug&quot;:[0,&quot;cyber-frontier-models&quot;],&quot;excerpt&quot;:[0,&quot;In recent weeks, we pointed Mythos and other security-focused LLMs at live code across critical parts of our infrastructure. We share what we observed, the modelsâ strengths and weaknesses, and what the work around them needs to look like before any of it can scale.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For the last few months, we&amp;#39;ve been testing a range of security-focused LLMs on our own infrastructure. These LLMs  help identify potential vulnerabilities in our own systems, so we can fix them â and they also show us what attackers are going to be able to do with the latest models.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;None of these LLMs has captured more attention than Mythos Preview, from Anthropic. A few weeks ago, we were invited to use Mythos Preview as part of &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.anthropic.com/glasswing\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Project Glasswing&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. We soon pointed it at more than fifty of our own repositories â to see what it would find, and to see how it works. &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This post shares what we observed, what the models did well and what they didn&amp;#39;t, and how the architecture and process around them needs to change, so they can be used at scale.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-changed-with-mythos-preview\&quot;&gt;What changed with Mythos Preview&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-changed-with-mythos-preview\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Mythos Preview is a real step forward, and it&amp;#39;s worth saying that plainly before getting into anything else. We&amp;#39;ve been running models against our code for a while now, and the jump from what was possible with previous general-purpose frontier models to what Mythos Preview does today is not just a refinement of what came before.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;It&amp;#39;s a different kind of tool doing a different kind of work, and that makes a clean apples-to-apples comparison to earlier models difficult. So rather than trying to benchmark Mythos Preview against general-purpose frontier models, it&amp;#39;s more useful to describe what it can actually do, and two features that stood out across the work we did with Mythos Preview:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Exploit chain construction -&lt;/b&gt; A real attack rarely uses one bug. It chains several small attack primitives together into a working exploit. For instance, it might turn a use-after-free bug into an arbitrary read and write primitive, hijack the control flow, and use return-oriented programming (ROP) chains to take full control over a system. Mythos Preview can take several of these primitives and reason about how to combine them into a working proof. The reasoning it shows along the way looks like the work of a senior researcher rather than the output of an automated scanner.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Proof generation -&lt;/b&gt; Finding a bug and proving it&amp;#39;s exploitable are two different things, and Mythos Preview can do both. It writes code that would trigger the suspected bug, compiles that code in a scratch environment, and runs it. If the program does what the model expected, that&amp;#39;s the proof. If it doesn&amp;#39;t, the model reads the failure, adjusts its hypothesis, and tries again. The loop matters as much as the bugs it finds, because a suspected flaw without a working proof is speculation, and Mythos Preview closes that gap on its own.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Some of what we describe above is not entirely unique to Mythos Preview. When we ran other frontier models through the same harness, they found a fair number of the same underlying bugs, and in some cases they got further than we expected on the reasoning side too. Where they fell short was at the point of stitching the pieces together. A model would identify an interesting bug, write a thoughtful description of why it mattered, and then stop, leaving the actual chain unfinished and the question of exploitability open. What changed with Mythos Preview is that a model can now take those low-severity bugs (which would traditionally sit invisible in a backlog) and chain them into a single, more severe exploit.Â &lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;model-refusals-in-legitimate-vulnerability-research\&quot;&gt;Model refusals in legitimate vulnerability research&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#model-refusals-in-legitimate-vulnerability-research\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The Mythos Preview model provided by Anthropic, as part of Project Glasswing, did not have the additional safeguards that are present in generally available models (like Opus 4.7 or GPT-5.5).&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Despite this, the model organically pushes back on certain requests - much like the cyber capabilities that made it useful for vulnerability hunting, the model has its own emergent guardrails that sometimes cause it to push back on legitimate security research requests. But as we found, these organic refusals arenât consistent - the same task, framed differently or presented in a different context, could produce completely different outcomes as illustrated in the examples below.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/5St6rLRq9wkuxwmLHZ88CV/b4eb948c917ef7f7b0028ccc8ec0aefe/image2.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1242\&quot; height=\&quot;716\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;Example of Mythos Preview pushing back on building a working proof of conceptÂ &lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;For example, the model initially refused to do vulnerability research on a project, then agreed to perform the same research on the same code after an unrelated change to the projectâs environment. Nothing about the code being analyzed had changed. \n\nIn another case, the model found and confirmed several serious memory bugs in a codebase, and then refused to write a demonstration exploit. The same request, framed differently, got a different answer, and even the same request can produce different outcomes across runs due to the probabilistic nature of the model. Semantically equivalent tasks can produce opposite outcomes depending on how and when theyâre presented to the model.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;This matters because while the modelâs organic refusals/guardrails are real, they arenât consistent enough to serve as a complete safety boundary on their own. Thatâs precisely why any capable cyber frontier model made generally available in the future must include additional safeguards on top of this baseline behavior - making it appropriate for broader use outside of a controlled research context like Project Glasswing.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;the-signal-to-noise-problem\&quot;&gt;The signal-to-noise problem&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-signal-to-noise-problem\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;One of the hardest parts of triaging security vulnerabilities is deciding which bugs are real, which are exploitable, and which need fixing now. This was a hard problem even in the pre-AI world. AI vulnerability scanners and AI-generated code have made it worse, and at Cloudflare we&amp;#39;ve built multiple post-validation stages to deal with it.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Two factors dominate the noise rate:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Programming language&lt;/b&gt; - C and C++ give you direct memory control and, with it, bug classes - buffer overflows, out-of-bounds reads and writes - that memory-safe languages like Rust eliminate at compile time. We saw consistently more false positives from projects written in memory-unsafe languages.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Model bias&lt;/b&gt; - A good human researcher tells you what they found and how confident they are. Models don&amp;#39;t. Ask a model to find bugs, and it will find them, whether the code has any or not. Findings come back hedged with &amp;quot;possibly,&amp;quot; &amp;quot;potentially,&amp;quot; &amp;quot;could in theory,&amp;quot; and the hedged findings vastly outnumber the solid ones. That&amp;#39;s a reasonable bias for an exploratory tool. It&amp;#39;s a ruinous one for a triage queue, where every speculative finding spends human attention and tokens to dismiss, and that cost compounds across thousands of findings.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Mythos Preview represents a clear improvement here, particularly in its ability to chain primitives - combining multiple vulnerabilities into a working proof of concept rather than reporting them in isolation. A finding that arrives with a PoC is a finding you can act on, and it means far less time spent asking &amp;quot;is this even real?&amp;quot;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our harnesses are deliberately tuned to over-report, so we see more (and miss less), which comes with a lot more noise. But at triage time, Mythos Preview&amp;#39;s output has noticeably higher quality: fewer hedged findings, clearer reproduction steps, and less work to reach a fix-or-dismiss decision.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;why-pointing-a-generic-coding-agent-at-a-repo-doesnt-work\&quot;&gt;Why pointing a generic coding agent at a repo doesn&amp;#39;t work&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#why-pointing-a-generic-coding-agent-at-a-repo-doesnt-work\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;When we first started AI-assisted vulnerability research last year, our instinct was the obvious one: point a generic coding agent at an arbitrary repository and ask it to discover vulnerabilities. This approach works, in the sense that the model will produce findings, but it doesn&amp;#39;t work in producing meaningful coverage of a real codebase and identifying findings of value. There are two main reasons for this:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Context -&lt;/b&gt; Coding agents are tuned for one focused stream of work: building a feature, fixing a bug, writing a refactor. They ingest a lot of source code, hold a single hypothesis at a time, and iterate against it. That&amp;#39;s exactly the wrong shape for vulnerability research, which is narrow and parallel by nature. A human researcher picks one specific thing to look at and investigates it thoroughly. That one thing might be a single complex feature, transitions across security boundaries, or a specific vulnerability class like command injections, where attacker input ends up being run as a shell command. Then they do it again, for a different feature, security boundary, or vulnerability class, several thousand times across the codebase. A single agent session (even with subagents) against a hundred-thousand-line repository can cover maybe a tenth of a percent of the surface in a useful way before the model&amp;#39;s context window fills up and compaction kicks in - potentially discarding earlier findings that would have mattered.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Throughput -&lt;/b&gt; A single-stream agent does one thing at a time, but real codebases need many hypotheses against many components at once, with the ability to fan out further when something interesting turns up. You can drive a single agent harder, but at some point you stop being limited by the model and start being limited by the shape of the interaction itself. Using the model directly in a coding agent turns out to be fine for manual investigation when a researcher already has a lead and wants a second pair of eyes. However, it&amp;#39;s the wrong tool for achieving high coverage. Once we accepted that, we stopped trying to make Mythos Preview do the wrong job and started building the harness around it instead.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-a-harness-actually-fixes\&quot;&gt;What a harness actually fixes&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-a-harness-actually-fixes\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Four lessons came out of running the work at scale, and each one pointed to the need for a harness that manages the overall execution:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Narrow scope produces better findings -&lt;/b&gt; Telling the model &amp;quot;Find vulnerabilities in this repository&amp;quot; makes it wander. Telling it &amp;quot;Look for command injection in this specific function, with this trust boundary above it, here&amp;#39;s the architecture document and here&amp;#39;s prior coverage of this area&amp;quot; makes it do something much closer to what a researcher would actually do.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Adversarial review reduces noise -&lt;/b&gt; Adding a second agent between the initial finding and the queue - one with a different prompt, a different model, and no ability to generate its own findings - catches a lot of the noise that the first agent would miss if it just checked its own work. It turns out that putting two agents in deliberate disagreement is way more effective than just telling one agent to be careful.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Splitting the chain across agents produces better reasoning -&lt;/b&gt; Asking &amp;quot;Is this code buggy?&amp;quot; and &amp;quot;Can an attacker actually reach this bug from outside the system?&amp;quot; are two different questions, and the model is better at each one when you ask them separately, because each question is narrower than the combined version.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Parallel narrow tasks beat one exhaustive agent -&lt;/b&gt; Coverage improves when many agents work on tightly scoped questions and we deduplicate the results afterward, rather than asking one agent to be exhaustive.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Each of those observations is about model behavior, and put together they describe something that isn&amp;#39;t a chat interface anymore. It&amp;#39;s a harness that helps you achieve the final outcomes. The first steps to building a harness are simple, as you can ask the model to help, which is what we did. We used Mythos Preview to build on, tailor, and improve our original harnesses to suit its strengths.\n\nAn example of what a harness looks like in practice is described below.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;our-vulnerability-discovery-harness\&quot;&gt;Our vulnerability discovery harness&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#our-vulnerability-discovery-harness\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Here&amp;#39;s what our vulnerability discovery harness looks like, stage by stage. It was used to scan live code across our runtime, edge data path, protocol stack, control plane, and the open-source projects we depend on.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/1OhgJDEeyc5aq8EoF4yFJE/917c9a9d8a92d0920acb96f7d5cb66f6/image6.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;905\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;figure class=\&quot;table table-style-block-align-left\&quot; style=\&quot;width:97.66%;\&quot;&gt;\n    &lt;table class=\&quot;ck-table-resized\&quot; style=\&quot;border-style:none;\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;colgroup&gt;&lt;col style=\&quot;width:11.55%;\&quot;&gt;&lt;col style=\&quot;width:56.06%;\&quot;&gt;&lt;col style=\&quot;width:32.39%;\&quot;&gt;&lt;/colgroup&gt;\n        &lt;tbody&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;background-color:#fce5cd;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Stage&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;background-color:#fce5cd;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What it does&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;background-color:#fce5cd;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Why it matters&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/5736CBrNDFnYglaRFUtGp0/2bccdc1e5b5f1b21416c6439cdb63967/BLOG-3301_image7.png 68w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 68px) 100vw, 68px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/5736CBrNDFnYglaRFUtGp0/2bccdc1e5b5f1b21416c6439cdb63967/BLOG-3301_image7.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;822rcAA5ctaT\&quot; width=\&quot;32\&quot; height=\&quot;32\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Recon&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;An agent reads the repository from the top down, fans out to subagents responsible for each subsystem, and produces an architecture document covering build commands, trust boundaries, entry points, and likely attack surface. It also generates the initial queue of tasks for the next stage.&amp;nbsp;&amp;nbsp;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Gives every downstream agent shared context. Cuts the wander problem.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7wGJ8jmh1gjMevxH31nSfv/b3bd6772cfa57939a1291cae8c4faab3/BLOG-3301_image8.png 68w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 68px) 100vw, 68px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7wGJ8jmh1gjMevxH31nSfv/b3bd6772cfa57939a1291cae8c4faab3/BLOG-3301_image8.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;MZetX0ROffYM\&quot; width=\&quot;31\&quot; height=\&quot;31\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&amp;nbsp;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Hunt&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Each task is one attack class paired with a scope hint. Hunters (the agents that actually look for bugs) run concurrently, typically around fifty at once, each fanning out to a handful of exploration subagents. Each hunter has access to tools that compile and run proof-of-concept code in a per-task scratch directory.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;This is where most of the work happens. Many narrow tasks in parallel, not one exhaustive agent.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7mMu2OOeot8CLuvu2PYAjx/144ecd5ef0785d8c6b0c7c3cd6feccc8/BLOG-3301_image5.png 68w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 68px) 100vw, 68px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7mMu2OOeot8CLuvu2PYAjx/144ecd5ef0785d8c6b0c7c3cd6feccc8/BLOG-3301_image5.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;b0ZpxOYkUhjr\&quot; width=\&quot;29\&quot; height=\&quot;29\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Validate&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;An independent agent re-reads the code and tries to disprove the original finding. It uses a different prompt and has no ability to emit new findings of its own.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Catches a meaningful fraction of the noise the hunter wouldn&#39;t catch when reviewing its own work.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7f8SJhTAja5bLPvydZkrv4/11a9cf6b0cee030db5f56d0dd46e0b45/BLOG-3301_image11.png 68w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 68px) 100vw, 68px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/7f8SJhTAja5bLPvydZkrv4/11a9cf6b0cee030db5f56d0dd46e0b45/BLOG-3301_image11.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;Y31tucc41jy0\&quot; width=\&quot;28\&quot; height=\&quot;28\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Gapfill&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Hunters flag areas they touched but didn&#39;t cover thoroughly. Those areas get re-queued for another pass.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Counteracts the model&#39;s tendency to drift toward attack classes it has already had success with.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/5tbCM9SUgCZlYF0m9e8CY0/c48b7f90e39ddc5bd6060d5067bae0ea/BLOG-3301_image4.png 51w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 51px) 100vw, 51px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/5tbCM9SUgCZlYF0m9e8CY0/c48b7f90e39ddc5bd6060d5067bae0ea/BLOG-3301_image4.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;lvNRuZ_yo_es\&quot; width=\&quot;28\&quot; height=\&quot;28\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Dedupe&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Findings that share the same root cause collapse into a single record.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Variant analysis is a feature, not a way to inflate the queue with duplicates.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/51VLQM1a40KKRdvq2HidRj/c9ad0209a54544fe783d9eb019a6f02b/BLOG-3301_image3.png 51w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 51px) 100vw, 51px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/51VLQM1a40KKRdvq2HidRj/c9ad0209a54544fe783d9eb019a6f02b/BLOG-3301_image3.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;_rUjvs8wRMj1\&quot; width=\&quot;29\&quot; height=\&quot;29\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Trace&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;For each confirmed finding in a shared library, a tracer agent fans out (one instance per consumer repository), uses a cross-repo symbol index, and decides whether attacker-controlled input actually reaches the bug from outside the system.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Turns \&quot;there is a flaw\&quot; into \&quot;there is a reachable vulnerability.\&quot; This is the stage that matters most.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/4UhAgIcdpKcWxNG2kO5fIN/6f883a0f993604339b5381b14f63f40d/BLOG-3301_image1.png 51w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 51px) 100vw, 51px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/4UhAgIcdpKcWxNG2kO5fIN/6f883a0f993604339b5381b14f63f40d/BLOG-3301_image1.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;ylBXj7IP-Thj\&quot; width=\&quot;26\&quot; height=\&quot;26\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Feedback&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Reachable traces become new hunt tasks in the consumer repositories where the bug is actually exposed.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Closes the loop. The pipeline gets better as it runs.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n            &lt;tr&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;picture&gt;&lt;source srcset=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/558dSiaH5GNrtmKGKbUEUH/8684108e9b99580e5194245a1e20cb79/BLOG-3301_image10.png 51w\&quot; type=\&quot;image/webp\&quot; sizes=\&quot;(max-width: 51px) 100vw, 51px\&quot;&gt;&lt;img src=\&quot;https://images.ctfassets.net/zkvhlag99gkb/558dSiaH5GNrtmKGKbUEUH/8684108e9b99580e5194245a1e20cb79/BLOG-3301_image10.png\&quot; data-ckbox-resource-id=\&quot;pDRKY2-VeUPg\&quot; width=\&quot;27\&quot; height=\&quot;27\&quot;&gt;&lt;/picture&gt;&lt;br&gt;\n                    Report&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;An agent writes a structured report against a predefined schema, fixes any validation errors against that schema itself, and submits the report to an ingest API.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n                &lt;td style=\&quot;border-color:#f6b26b;border-width:1pt;padding:5pt;vertical-align:top;\&quot;&gt;\n                    &lt;span style=\&quot;background-color:transparent;color:#000000;font-family:Inter,sans-serif;font-size:15px;\&quot;&gt;Output is queryable data, not free-form prose.&lt;/span&gt;\n                &lt;/td&gt;\n            &lt;/tr&gt;\n        &lt;/tbody&gt;\n    &lt;/table&gt;\n&lt;/figure&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;what-this-means-for-security-teams\&quot;&gt;What this means for security teams&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#what-this-means-for-security-teams\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The loudest reaction to Mythos Preview from other security leaders has been about speed - scan faster, patch faster, compress the response cycle. More than one team we have spoken with is now operating under a two-hour SLA from CVE release to patch in production. The instinct is understandable: when the attacker timeline shortens, the defender timeline has to shorten with it. Faster is not going to be enough, and we think a lot of teams are about to spend a lot of time, effort, and money learning that the hard way.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Patching faster does not change the shape of the pipeline that produces the patch. If regression testing takes a day, you cannot get to a two-hour SLA without skipping it, and the bugs you ship when you skip regression testing tend to be worse than the bugs you were trying to patch. We learned a version of this when we tried letting the model write its own patches and watched a few go out that fixed the original bug while quietly breaking something else the code depended on.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The harder question is what the architecture around the vulnerability should look like. The principle is to make exploitation harder for an attacker even when a bug exists, so that the gap between when a vulnerability is disclosed and when it is patched matters less. That means defenses that sit in front of the application and block the bug from being reached. It means designing the application so that a flaw in one part of the code cannot give an attacker access to other parts. It means being able to roll out a fix to every place the code is running at the same moment, rather than waiting on individual teams to deploy it.Â &lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;We also recognize this topic cuts both ways. The same capabilities that helped us find bugs in our own code will, in the wrong hands, accelerate the attack side against every application on the Internet. Cloudflare sits in front of millions of those applications, and the architectural principles described above are exactly the ones our products are built to apply on behalf of customers. We will share more on what that means for customers in the weeks ahead.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If your team is doing similar work and would like to compare notes, reach out to us at &lt;a href=\&quot;#\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;security-ai-research@cloudflare.com&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;i&gt;Our research with Mythos Preview was conducted in a controlled environment against our own code; every vulnerability surfaced through this work was triaged, validated, and remediated where action was needed under Cloudflare&amp;#39;s formal vulnerability management process.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;i&gt;This work was a team effort. Thanks to Albert Pedersen, Craig Strubhart, Dan Jones, Irtefa Fairuz, Martin Schwarzl, and Rohit Chenna Reddy for their contributions to the research, engineering, and analysis behind this blog This means builders can scale agent workflows globally while strictly controlling access to private backends and easily customizing their agentâs tools and runtimes.&quot;],&quot;featured&quot;:[0,false],&quot;html&quot;:[0,&quot;&lt;p&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Cloudflare and Anthropic have collaborated to integrate Claude Managed Agents with Cloudflare Sandboxes. Our &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/sandbox/tutorials/claude-managed-agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;new integration&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; gives you more control over your agent sandboxes, secures connections to private services, and improves observability.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;In the past year, Cloudflareâs Developer Platform has expanded to give more developers the tools they need to run agents at scale. This includes:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/sandbox/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Sandboxes&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; for full stateful Linux microVMs at scale&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Agents SDK&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, providing simple and customizable agent framework&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/browser-run/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Browser Run&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, which gives agents fully programmable and observable browsers&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Dynamic Workers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, allowing for dynamic sandboxed code execution at massive scale&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;Our goal is to make Cloudflare the simplest, most secure, and most programmable cloud for agents.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Integrating with Claude Managed Agents is another step in this direction. You can run your agent loop on the Claude Platform, while using Cloudflare to execute code, secure connections, and run custom tool calls.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;To get going in just minutes, weâve created a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/claude-managed-agents\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;default deployment template &lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;that gives you the following:&lt;/p&gt;&lt;ul&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Enhanced security&lt;/b&gt; - Run all agent traffic through customizable proxies. This allows you to securely inject credentials, prevent data exfiltration, and better observe how your agents interact with the outside world.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Sandbox control and observability&lt;/b&gt; - Get detailed sandbox metrics and logs. SSH into running machines. Customize sandbox images.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Lightweight sandboxes&lt;/b&gt; - Writing and executing untrusted code can be done in a traditional microVM or a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;lightweight isolate&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. This lets you hit massive scale, boot sandboxes in milliseconds, and minimize infrastructure spend.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Private service connectivity&lt;/b&gt; - Connect agents to private internal services without ever exposing them to the Internet.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Browser Control and Observability&lt;/b&gt; - Get an audit trail of every agentâs browser sessions, including session recording and human-in-the-loop flows.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Email &lt;/b&gt;- Give each of your agents its own email address and ability to send emails.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;li&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;b&gt;Custom tools&lt;/b&gt; - Extend your agents with tools without needing additional infrastructure. Just write functions and deploy.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;/li&gt;&lt;/ul&gt;&lt;p&gt;You get all of this out of the box when deploying the integration, and you can easily customize if you need more.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Letâs take a brief look at Claude Managed Agents, see how to integrate a Cloudflare-based environment, then explore how to get the most out of Claude on Cloudflare.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;an-overview-of-claude-managed-agents\&quot;&gt;An overview of Claude Managed Agents&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#an-overview-of-claude-managed-agents\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;&lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.anthropic.com/engineering/managed-agents\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Claude Managed Agents&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; allow developers to easily define and run agents on the Anthropic platform. In these managed environments, Claude can read files, run commands, browse the web, and execute code. The harness supports built-in prompt caching, compaction, and various agent-first performance optimizations.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Until now, using Claude Managed Agents has meant running the entire stack on Anthropic-provided infrastructure. While this is great for some developers, others may need more control over their infrastructure choice, whether this is for security, compliance, or performance reasons. Self-managed environments for Claude Agents provide just that.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Anthropic describes this as âdecoupling the brain from the hands.â The core agent loop runs in Anthropic (the âbrainâ), but the infrastructure for running and executing code (the âhandsâ) can be run anywhere, including Cloudflare.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;the-cloudflare-environment\&quot;&gt;The Cloudflare environment&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#the-cloudflare-environment\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Our new integration gives your agents a Cloudflare-based environment for running and executing code within minutes.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Follow &lt;a href=\&quot;https://github.com/cloudflare/claude-managed-agents#onboarding-guide\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;the onboarding guide&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to get started. Then fork the repo and customize your integration as you see fit.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;After setup, when a Claude Agent starts a session, it sends a message to your new Cloudflare-based control plane. The &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;-based control plane gives each agent session a sandboxed environment for executing code, developing applications, running CLI tools, and more. State is automatically persisted across session sleeps.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/6nYyreZYWyaKo6Xf2XjmtM/5cc21576a83e223a2cf67e8ed3fde928/image5.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;Sandboxes write files and execute code in response to the Claude-based Agent loop\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1070\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;Sandboxes write files and execute code in response to the Claude-based Agent loop&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can optionally configure sandbox instance sizes or customize the container image that runs within VM-based sandboxes. Each sandbox can be observed in the Cloudflare dashboard, sandbox logs can be queried or shipped to external providers like Datadog or Splunk, and the control plane ships with a built-in UI, making it easy to track the state of sandboxes or SSH into specific machines.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/46pEZh7MeAt27wLi7c9e6S/889b269b94db32c3188e3dff5b6cbd75/image3.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1439\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;Get interactive shell sessions into your agentâs sandbox&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;enabling-agents-at-internet-scale\&quot;&gt;Enabling agents at Internet scale&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#enabling-agents-at-internet-scale\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;What if your agent backend booted in a few milliseconds, and you didnât have to pay for the resources of a full VM when running the agent?&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The industry needs &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/#dynamic-worker-loader-a-lean-sandbox\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;a lightweight primitive for sandboxing&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; as we adopt agents at scale, and weâre building just that.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But as models get better, we expect more and more workflows to be managed by agents. Each of your customers should be able to run many agents simultaneously; each of your employees should have tens of agents running at once. If weâre constantly running a full microVM per agent, weâll be unnecessarily burning a ton of resources and money to enable this scale.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Thatâs why weâre providing a faster and cheaper sandbox for your Claude Agents. This sandbox is based on the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;AgentsSDK&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. You can execute arbitrary code in &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Dynamic Workers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; using &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/agents/api-reference/codemode/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Codemode&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, and you still &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/sandbox/bridge/http-api/#workspace-persistence\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;get a file system&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, but your agent is doing all of this within a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers/reference/how-workers-works/#isolates\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;V8 isolate&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; instead of a microVM.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you need agents to act as a developer, building full applications and running Linux-based tools, you can still reach for a microVM-based sandbox. For this, we provide &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/containers/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Containers&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, which Claude Managed Agents can also use.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;But if you want a faster, cheaper, and more scalable alternative you can use isolates instead of microVMs easily. Just select âisolateâ for backend type when setting up an Agent.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/MZ8cQ2UTYcbe2hFv7IUYw/f7b55a65b6a670651bf30a0e24a6f509/image1.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1285\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;Setting up an âisolateâ backend gives you a lightweight V8 isolate sandbox instead of a microVM&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;If you want to handle bursts of tens of thousands of concurrent agents or more, running with isolates will allow you to scale in a way that no VM-based solution allows.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;securing-your-agentic-workloads\&quot;&gt;Securing your agentic workloads&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#securing-your-agentic-workloads\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agents are far more powerful when they connect to your organizationâs context. This usually means accessing private services and data.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/1LXewj3QO0QZMiEYNEMVxk/08cf093a16e67c8fb48514b1ce744319/image9.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1496\&quot; height=\&quot;736\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;As &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/sandbox-auth/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;weâve written before&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, sandboxed workloads on Cloudflare can use an outbound proxy for fully dynamic, customizable, and zero-trust authentication between sandboxes and external services. This lets you inject secrets into requests outside the sandbox, so the agent never has access to them. This protects against exfiltration attacks.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;And sometimes internal services shouldnât ever be exposed to the open Internet. We recently launched &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/cloudflare-one/networks/connectors/cloudflare-mesh/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Mesh&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/workers-vpc/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Workers VPC&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to better connect to these private services, whether theyâre running on a cloud provider like AWS or on-premises. This allows you to connect to internal services using post-quantum encrypted networking without a VPN or bastion host.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Claude Managed Agents can easily connect to private services with header injection or private VPC/Mesh tunnels. This is done via customizable outbound proxies. You can define egress policies that expose only the services you choose to the agent sandboxes that you choose. You can allowlist specific endpoints, perform zero-trust injection of encrypted credentials, access private services via Cloudflare Mesh, and even write custom proxy middleware.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/2728IFBEK7MUx4YDuIldFj/2e28b1fd510abf49dd3a5ad6604b7709/image4.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1552\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;The integration uses outbound Workers to handle egress however you see fit&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Youâre able to apply policies per tenant, per agent, or based on whatever metadata is useful. This gives you full control over how your agents connect to external services.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h2 id=\&quot;doing-more-with-the-cloudflare-developer-platform\&quot;&gt;Doing more with the Cloudflare Developer Platform&lt;/h2&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#doing-more-with-the-cloudflare-developer-platform\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Agents need more than just a code execution environment. Cloudflareâs Developer Platform provides the tools you need by default to let your agents do more.&lt;/p&gt;\n          &lt;figure class=\&quot;kg-card kg-image-card\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;Image src=\&quot;https://cf-assets.www.cloudflare.com/zkvhlag99gkb/7GkOWWKBsbnBYeaIcrbHrm/e385f4fa3d8ca2fff13a92a92854f94d/image7.png\&quot; alt=\&quot;\&quot; class=\&quot;kg-image\&quot; width=\&quot;1999\&quot; height=\&quot;1057\&quot; loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;/&gt;\n          &lt;/figure&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;Sandboxes can make tool calls on Cloudflare and safely access external services.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Here are a few of the tools youâll find most useful as you deploy agents on Cloudflare:&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;browser-run-via-claude\&quot;&gt;Browser Run via Claude&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#browser-run-via-claude\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;One of the most common tools agents need is a browser. While &lt;code&gt;curl&lt;/code&gt; can get you pretty far, when you want an agent to act like a human, this often means interacting with the web like one: rendering JS-heavy applications, taking screenshots for QA validation, filling out forms, etc. &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/browser-run-for-ai-agents/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Browser Run&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; is Cloudflareâs tool to give agents browsers.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;div style=\&quot;position: relative; padding-top: 52.94233687405159%;\&quot;&gt;\n  &lt;iframe\n    src=\&quot;https://customer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com/304a6ff2a6b8b310be4c421ea7d77ca4/iframe?muted=true&amp;preload=true&amp;loop=true&amp;autoplay=true&amp;poster=https%3A%2F%2Fcustomer-eq7kiuol0tk9chox.cloudflarestream.com%2F304a6ff2a6b8b310be4c421ea7d77ca4%2Fthumbnails%2Fthumbnail.jpg%3Ftime%3D%26height%3D600&amp;primaryColor=%23f6821f\&quot;\n    loading=\&quot;lazy\&quot;\n    style=\&quot;border: none; position: absolute; top: 0; left: 0; height: 100%; width: 100%;\&quot;\n    allow=\&quot;accelerometer; gyroscope; autoplay; encrypted-media; picture-in-picture;\&quot;\n    allowfullscreen=\&quot;true\&quot;\n  &gt;&lt;/iframe&gt;\n&lt;/div&gt;\n&lt;p&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;&lt;sup&gt;&lt;i&gt;A Browser Run session recording lets you watch how your agents used a browser. One of many built-in tools.&lt;/i&gt;&lt;/sup&gt;&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;The Claude Managed Agents integration ships with multiple browser-related tools that can be enabled immediately. These include &lt;code&gt;browser_search&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;browser_execute&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;screenshot&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;browse&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;fetch_to_markdown&lt;/code&gt;, and a Cloudflare-specific implementation of &lt;code&gt;web_fetch&lt;/code&gt; allows your agent to control a browser that runs on Cloudflare infrastructure. This not only lets your agent do more, but it also makes it easy to audit every action your agentâs browser is taking on the web, apply allowlists and denylist to browser sessions, and save recordings of browser sessions for future debugging.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;agent-inboxes\&quot;&gt;Agent inboxes&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#agent-inboxes\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;The integration also comes with built-in support for email with the &lt;code&gt;send_email&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;email_read&lt;/code&gt;, and &lt;code&gt;email_list&lt;/code&gt; tools.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;You can also kick off new sessions via email, or configure the agent to send emails using any domain and address configured with the &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/email-for-agents/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflare Email Service&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. This allows the agent to act on your behalf when it needs to, reply to context in forwarded emails, and autonomously interact with others via email.&lt;/p&gt;\n    &lt;div class=\&quot;flex anchor relative\&quot;&gt;\n      &lt;h3 id=\&quot;custom-tools-and-more\&quot;&gt;Custom tools and more&lt;/h3&gt;\n      &lt;a href=\&quot;#custom-tools-and-more\&quot; aria-hidden=\&quot;true\&quot; class=\&quot;relative sm:absolute sm:-start-5\&quot;&gt;\n        &lt;svg width=\&quot;16\&quot; height=\&quot;16\&quot; viewBox=\&quot;0 0 24 24\&quot;&gt;&lt;path fill=\&quot;currentcolor\&quot; d=\&quot;m12.11 15.39-3.88 3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1-3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0-3.5l3.88-3.88a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-3.88 3.89a4.48 4.48 0 0 0 6.33 6.33l3.89-3.88a1 1 0 1 0-1.42-1.42Zm8.58-12.08a4.49 4.49 0 0 0-6.33 0l-3.89 3.88a1 1 0 0 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.88a2.52 2.52 0 0 1 3.5 0 2.47 2.47 0 0 1 0 3.5l-3.88 3.88a1 1 0 1 0 1.42 1.42l3.88-3.89a4.49 4.49 0 0 0 0-6.33ZM8.83 15.17a1 1 0 0 0 1.1.22 1 1 0 0 0 .32-.22l4.92-4.92a1 1 0 0 0-1.42-1.42l-4.92 4.92a1 1 0 0 0 0 1.42Z\&quot;&gt;&lt;/path&gt;&lt;/svg&gt;\n      &lt;/a&gt;\n    &lt;/div&gt;\n    &lt;p&gt;Other built-in tools include &lt;code&gt;call_service&lt;/code&gt;, which uses Cloudflare Mesh or Workers VPC to connect to private services, and &lt;code&gt;image_generate&lt;/code&gt;, which uses &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/products/workers-ai/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers AI&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt; to generate images on Cloudflare. This pairs well with Claude providing text-based inference.&lt;/p&gt;&lt;p&gt;Additionally, we encourage forking the repo to easily add customized tools. For example, you could add a custom tool to host a public file on &lt;a href=\&quot;https://www.cloudflare.com/developer-platform/r2/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Cloudflareâs R2 object storage&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;. Just add the relevant binding in wrangler config, write a &lt;a href=\&quot;https://zod.dev/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;code&gt;&lt;u&gt;zod&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/a&gt; definition, and short function in &lt;code&gt;custom-tools.js&lt;/code&gt;:&lt;/p&gt;\n            &lt;pre class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;&lt;code class=\&quot;language-JavaScript\&quot;&gt;defineTool({\n  name: &amp;quot;r2_host_file&amp;quot;,\n  description: &amp;quot;Upload from sandbox to R2 and get a public URL.&amp;quot;,\n  inputSchema: z.object({\n    key: z.string().describe(&amp;quot;Object key&amp;quot;),\n    content: z.string().describe(&amp;quot;UTF-8 file body&amp;quot;),\n    contentType: z.string().describe(&amp;quot;MIME type&amp;quot;),\n  }),\n  run: async ({ key, content, contentType }, { env }) =&amp;gt; {\n    await env.PUBLIC_BUCKET.put(\n      key, content, { httpMetadata: { contentType }}\n    );\n    return `${env.PUB_R2_URL.replace(/\\/$/, &amp;quot;&amp;quot;)}/${encodeURI(key)}`;\n  }\n}),&lt;/pre&gt;&lt;/code&gt;\n            &lt;p&gt;The Cloudflare Developer Platform provides all sorts of possibilities for extending your agents: give each agent session a git-backed repo with &lt;a href=\&quot;https://blog.cloudflare.com/artifacts-git-for-agents-beta/\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Artifacts&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, run edge inference with &lt;a href=\&quot;https://workers.cloudflare.com/products/workers-ai/\&quot; target=\&quot;_blank\&quot; rel=\&quot;noopener noreferrer\&quot;&gt;&lt;u&gt;Workers AI&lt;/u&gt;&lt;/a&gt;, host applications written on the fly with &lt;a href=\&quot;https://developers.cloudflare.com/dynamic-workers/\&quot;
